# Session log

> Append-only diary of every working session on this project. Updated by the responding model **after every task completion**, not at session-end. The log is the audit trail an auditor (or a future session) reads to know what changed when, by whom, and why.

## Method — when and how to append

A new entry is appended:

1. **After every `TaskUpdate` flips a task to `completed`** in the harness task list.
2. **After every accepted ADR** (cross-reference the ADR number in the entry).
3. **After every closed risk** (cross-reference the R-NNN id).
4. **After every meaningful file create / move / delete** that affects the design surface (the prompt, the deliverable, or any `plans/` file).
5. **After every clarifying-question / answer exchange with the user** where the answer became a decision (ADR or §-anchor edit).

The entry shape is fixed. Use the template at the bottom. The columns are mandatory; freeform notes go in the bullets below the table row.

## Method — what an entry contains

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| `id` | `S-NNN-T-MM` — session number, task number within session (e.g. `S-001-T-04`). Monotonic. Durable across sessions. |
| `timestamp` | ISO-8601 UTC. The time the *task completed*, not when the entry was appended. |
| `actor` | `user@host` of the operator; for AI-driven entries, `model:claude-opus-4-7@user@host` (the responding model + user). |
| `surface` | The surface where the work happened: `prompt`, `architecture`, `plans`, `code`, `kit`, `audit`. |
| `action` | One-line imperative verb-phrase. "Added §43 Testing Strategy" — not "I added §43". |
| `files_touched` | List of files (relative to the repo root) the action created / modified / deleted. |
| `grounding` | (added in v4.4 per ADR-030) List of files the model READ before performing the action — the basis for any factual claims in the action. Proves anti-hallucination compliance. Empty only for trivial actions like "ran `wc -l`". |
| `linked` | ADR-NNN refs, R-NNN risk refs, T-NNN checklist refs, S-NNN session refs, prompt §-anchors. |
| `audit_chain` | (Optional) The `entry_hash` from the global `.sange/audit/` log entry that mirrors this session-log row, when the project is far enough along that the runtime audit chain is producing entries. |
| `notes` | Freeform bullets — *why*, edge cases, follow-ups discovered. Use `🟡 UNVERIFIED`, `✅ Verified`, `❌ Refuted` markers per ADR-030. |

## Method — audit after every step

The session-log row **is** the audit step. There is no separate "audit-after-task" step beyond appending this row. The integrity comes from:

- The log being append-only (no edits to prior rows; corrections go in a new row referencing the prior `id`).
- The `linked` column tying every entry to a verifiable artifact elsewhere (ADR row, risk-register row, prompt §-anchor, checklist task, git commit hash when applicable).
- The `audit_chain` column (when present) linking the design-time row to the runtime `.sange/audit/` JSONL chain, so the same operator's design decisions and runtime actions are correlated.

Once the v0.1 code is up and the daemon is producing real audit JSONL, the session-log can be emitted automatically from those entries via `tools/generators/session_log.py` (a future T-G generator) — until then, manual append is the rule.

## Method — checklist integration

When a session-log entry corresponds to a `checklist.md` task completion, the entry's `linked` column **must** name the task ID (e.g. `T-001`, `T-G-005`). The reverse pointer — from `checklist.md` to the session-log row that closed it — is added by editing the task description with `(closed in S-NNN-T-MM)`. This makes the checklist's history machine-readable.

When a task is partially complete (real work happened but acceptance criteria not yet green), a session-log entry **may** still be appended with `action: progress` and `linked: T-NNN (in-progress)`. The checklist task stays unchecked.

## Method — fork-friendliness (per ADR-025 godmode workbook)

This file's *structure* is reusable for any future agency project. The Sange-specific entries below get truncated/deleted when forking; the **method** sections above stay. Future projects copy the template, increment session numbers from `S-001-T-01`, and rely on the same audit shape.

Three-step streamed pipeline, each step landing one audit chain entry with a 0600 transcript via T-110's `run_streamed`: (1) `git rev-list --all --objects` enumerates every reachable (sha, path) pair across every ref; the parser builds `path → set[sha]` from `[stdout] ` lines of the transcript. (2) `git cat-file --batch-all-objects --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectsize) %(objectname)'` — the cleverness: `--batch-all-objects` makes git enumerate every object in the repo *without* needing stdin (which `run_streamed` doesn't support yet — keeping T-110 scope-clean per CLAUDE.md). For each line `<type> <size> <sha>`, we keep only blob-typed shas that intersect with the candidate set from step 1, summing sizes as we go. (3) `git log --all --pretty=format:%H -- <matched_paths>` collects every commit-sha that ever touched a matched path; deduped to `affected_commits` count. **Filter semantics** mirror the spec's `filters` field shape: `plan.filters.paths` (exact-match set intersection) + `plan.filters.globs` (`fnmatch.fnmatch` per glob across every enumerated path). `replace_text_hashes` is NOT applied here — it's a *redaction* filter consumed by the destructive rewrite tool (v1.0+ T-203), not a path filter. **Subprocess elision when nothing matches**: if `matched_paths` is empty, the `git log` step is skipped entirely (`log_event_id = ""`, `affected_commits = 0`). Avoids invoking `git log` with no pathspec (which would silently return every commit in the repo and lie about the count). This means the chain has 2 events instead of 3 in the no-match case — both behaviors covered by separate tests. **`AnalysisResult`** frozen dataclass: `affected_commits` + `matched_blob_shas` (sorted tuple — canonical for cross-run hash stability) + `matched_paths` (sorted tuple) + `size_delta_bytes` (negative integer; bytes that would be reclaimed) + three `event_id` fields. `.deleted_objects` property = `len(matched_blob_shas)`. `.as_counts()` returns the §6.11.6 `counts` dict shape (`{affected_commits, deleted_objects, size_delta_bytes}`) ready to merge into `plan.counts` — `affected_refs` is intentionally absent from this slice because per-ref reachability needs N×M `merge-base --is-ancestor` calls and lands in T-111d alongside the preflight gates. **`AnalysisError`** raised on non-git VCS / missing mirror dir / non-zero subprocess exit (with transcript path in message for forensic recovery). **End-to-end smoke** against an ephemeral source repo with 4 commits (c1: readme.md / c2: secret.txt v1 / c3: secret.txt v2 — different bytes, different blob sha / c4: notes/keys.pem) → `create_mirror(source)` → `analyze_mirror(plan, mirror, filters=PurgeFilters(paths=["secret.txt"]))` returns `deleted_objects=2` (v1 + v2 = 2 distinct blob shas), `affected_commits=2` (c2 + c3), `size_delta_bytes=-32` (-(11 + 21) bytes), `matched_paths=("secret.txt",)`. Glob `notes/*` matches `notes/keys.pem` (1 blob, 1 commit, -16 bytes). `paths + globs` union case: `paths=["secret.txt"], globs=["notes/*"]` → 3 blobs across 3 distinct commits. **Tests** (+15): `TestAnalyzeMirrorExactPath × 4` (two-versions-of-secret / affected_commits=2 / size_delta_bytes=-32 / no-match-returns-zeros + empty log_event_id + skipped log subprocess); `TestAnalyzeMirrorGlob × 3` (glob-matches-subdir / glob-matches-extension via `*.pem` / paths+globs unioned); `TestAnalyzeMirrorCounts × 4` (as_counts dict shape with no `affected_refs` / fresh-chain has 3 events when matched / fresh-chain has 2 events when no match / event ids distinct); `TestAnalyzeMirrorErrors × 2` (non-git rejected / missing mirror raises); `TestAnalysisResultProperties × 2` (deleted_objects property / matched_blob_shas sorted). I considered three paths: (a) extend `run_streamed` to take a `stdin: str | None = None` parameter — small expansion of T-110's API surface; (b) shell out to per-sha `git cat-file -s <sha>` + `-t <sha>` — N + N subprocesses, fine for small purges but quadratic-feeling for the operator; (c) use `--batch-all-objects` which enumerates EVERY object in the repo without stdin — one subprocess regardless of candidate count. Picked (c) because: (i) it keeps T-110's API surface frozen per CLAUDE.md scope discipline; (ii) the cost is bounded by repo size, not by filter complexity (a 1M-object repo produces ~60MB of cat-file output, well within memory); (iii) it's the same shape git-filter-repo's `--analyze` uses internally per its source code; (iv) any future need for "scan specific shas with type checks" can re-evaluate stdin-feeding then. **What I intentionally did NOT do in this slice**: `affected_refs` (per-ref reachability — defers to T-111d when the preflight gates need it anyway); `--dry-run` of `git filter-repo` (that's a destructive-ish call that v0.5 explicitly defers per the v0.5 scope row — the analyzer is sufficient for "preview impact"; `--dry-run` lands when destructive ops do, in v1.0+); scanner pre-run (gate 8 — `gitleaks` + `trufflehog` against the mirror; defers to T-111d). **The analyzer is independent of the CLI**: any future caller (T-111e CLI, Web UI plan-detail page, MCP tool) can run the analysis by composing PurgePlan + mirror_path + AuditChain. The model + analyzer + mirror are all unit-testable in isolation. |

Pipeline: (1) reject non-git VCS (`plan.target_vcs != "git"` raises `MirrorError` — SVN/Hg/P4 mirrors land in v1.0+ when the destructive subsystem itself does); (2) compute destination as `<repo_root>/.sange/purge/<plan_id>/work.git` (sibling to `plan.json`); (3) refuse-clobber if dest exists (operator must `rm -rf` to retry — eliminates the "stale half-cloned mirror reused as fresh" foot-gun); (4) resolve source URL via override > `plan.target_repo.remote` > `file://<repo_root>` fallback (last-resort lets a local repo be its own clone source for v0.5 dev / testing); (5) `run_streamed(["git", "clone", "--mirror", source, dest], event_kind=GENERIC, payload={phase: "mirror-clone"})` — clone subprocess output streams to transcript + audit chain; (6) on non-zero clone exit, `MirrorError` with transcript path; (7) `run_streamed(["git", "--git-dir", dest, "fsck", "--full", "--strict", "--no-progress"])` — fsck runs against the mirror; non-zero exit raises with transcript path; (8) `run_streamed(["git", "--git-dir", dest, "for-each-ref", "--format=%(objectname) %(refname)"])` — captures the baseline ref-set; (9) parse refs from the transcript's `[stdout] ` prefix (not from any streaming-helper stdout capture — the helper writes everything to transcript, that's the single source of truth); (10) sort by ref name + return canonical-tuple. `verify_mirror(plan, repo_root, *, audit_chain, actor, baseline_refs, timeout=120)` → `MirrorVerification`: re-runs `for-each-ref` against the mirror, diffs against `baseline_refs`. Returns `passed` + `added_refs` (now-present-not-in-baseline) + `removed_refs` (was-in-baseline-now-absent) + `changed_refs` (tuple of `(ref, old_sha, new_sha)` for refs in both with diverged shas) + `current_event_id`. Implements the §6.11 Red-Team #2 mitigation: "concurrent push by another collaborator lands between `analyzed` and `executing` — re-`--analyze` and re-confirm before executing". **`MirrorResult`** frozen dataclass: `path` (Path) + `source_url` (str) + `clone_event_id` + `fsck_event_id` + `fsck_passed` (bool) + `refs` (tuple[(ref, sha)]) + `ref_count`. **`MirrorVerification`** frozen dataclass: `passed` + `added_refs` + `removed_refs` + `changed_refs` + `current_event_id`. **`MirrorError`** raised at every "this can't proceed" failure mode (destination exists / non-git VCS / non-zero subprocess exit / missing mirror on verify). **End-to-end smoke**: ephemeral source repo with 2 commits + 1 tag + 1 branch via `git init` (initial-branch=main) → `create_mirror` writes `.sange/purge/<plan_id>/work.git/` (bare layout — `HEAD` + `objects/` + `refs/`, no `.git/`) → `refs` tuple has exactly 3 entries (`refs/heads/main`, `refs/heads/feature/x`, `refs/tags/v1.0`) → fsck_passed=True → chain has 3 events with phases `mirror-clone` / `mirror-fsck` / `mirror-for-each-ref` and prev_hash linkage threading through → calling `create_mirror` again raises `MirrorError("already exists")` → injecting a ref via `git update-ref refs/heads/injected <head_sha>` against the mirror → `verify_mirror(baseline_refs=initial_refs)` returns passed=False with `"refs/heads/injected"` in `added_refs`. **Tests** (+14): `TestCreateMirror` × 9 (creates mirror dir / refs match source 3 refs / fsck_passed / audit-chain-has-3-events with prev_hash threading + correct phases / event-kind GENERIC / refuses-to-clobber / non-git rejected / bad-source-URL raises with "exited" / source-URL falls back to plan.target_repo.remote when override absent); `TestVerifyMirror` × 5 (unchanged passes / removed-ref detected via synthetic baseline with extra ref / changed-ref detected via faked sha baseline / missing-mirror raises / added-ref-after-create via real `git update-ref` injection). Module-level `pytestmark = [skipif win32, skipif no git]`. **One mid-impl ruff polish**: RUF005 `result.refs + (("refs/heads/fake-baseline", "0"*40),)` → tuple-unpacking `(*result.refs, ("refs/heads/fake-baseline", "0"*40))`. **Four gates green**: ruff 0, mypy 0 (73 → 74 source files), pytest **1578 → 1592 passing** (+14 from T-111b), generators ok=12 stale=0. **Phase 2 progress**: T-100 ✓, T-101 ✓, T-102 ✓, T-103 ✓, T-108 ✓, T-110 ✓, T-111a ✓, **T-111b ✓** (mirror clone). 7 of ~15 v0.5 tasks closed. **What this unblocks**: T-111c (analyzer — runs `git rev-list --all --objects` against the MIRROR not the working repo, populates `plan.counts`); T-111d (the §6.11.4 preflight gates — backup tarball, branch-protection inventory, CI pause, collaborator-notification template, scanner pre-run); T-111e (CLI surface `sange purge mirror|plan|list|show|analyze|abort`). Backup tarball (gate 3) is a small follow-on slice that should be paired with the analyzer slice since both operate against the mirror produced here. **Architectural decision worth recording**: the streaming helper writes its capture to the transcript file FIRST, and consumers read parseable output BACK from the transcript via the `[stdout] ` line prefix. I considered returning `stdout_text` / `stderr_text` from `run_streamed` for convenience, but rejected it: (a) doubles the memory footprint for large subprocess outputs (clone of a 5 GB repo); (b) introduces two-sources-of-truth (the in-memory string + the transcript file) that could drift if a `line_callback` mutates state mid-call; (c) the transcript is the spec-mandated retention surface — making it canonical means the audit chain + the parser see the same bytes. **Unpushed commits on local main: 1** (this commit). The downside is the CLI MUST not forget to chain — that's enforced by the §6.11.6 invariant "every state transition produces a JSONL line" + a future linter / static check. (2) **Plan-id format with dashes-instead-of-colons.** The audit event_id format in §6.11.6 example uses `purge-2026-05-13T14-32-18Z-abc123` (dashes inside the timestamp). I considered using ISO-with-colons (`T14:32:18`) for human readability + going colon-free only on Windows. Rejected because (a) Windows filesystems forbid `:` in basenames and the plan-id appears as a *directory name* (`.sange/purge/<plan-id>/`), not just a JSON field; (b) URL escaping of `:` in URLs that reference the plan-id (web UI plan-detail page) is brittle; (c) the lex-sort property of the dashes-only form gives us "chronological list by default" via plain `sorted()` without a custom comparator. **What this unblocks**: every subsequent T-111 slice — T-111b mirror clone needs a `PurgePlan` to anchor; T-111c analyzer reads `plan.filters` + writes `plan.counts` + `plan.scanner_results`; T-111d preflight runs each §6.11.4 gate against the plan + appends to `plan.preflight_checks`; T-111e CLI exposes `sange purge plan` / `list` / `show <id>` / `analyze <id>` / `abort <id>`. ALL of those can ship without changing this slice's surface — the model is API-stable. |

The async core spawns via `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec(stdout=PIPE, stderr=PIPE)`, creates two `asyncio.create_task(reader(...))` workers over each pipe, gathers both, and writes a single combined `[stream] line` per readline (single Python call → single bytecode → no slicing between readers when the asyncio scheduler yields between two writes). All four honor `--json`. Wired into `main.py` via `app.add_typer(audit_app, name="audit", ...)`. **End-to-end smoke**: empty repo → `sange audit verify` reports `0 records / 0 shards` exit 0; append 3 events → verify clean (3 records / 1 shard); mid-chain `actor` mutation on disk → verify fails with `hash-mismatch` at `failure_index=1` exit 1. **Tests** (+50): `test_audit_event.py` (15 — EventKind values + make_event + compute_hash determinism + JSON round-trip + immutability), `test_audit_chain.py` (9 — append + last_hash + ISO-week sharding + iter_events + count), `test_audit_verify.py` (11 — clean / empty / malformed / mid-chain mutation / broken prev_hash / multi-shard / cross-shard linkage), `test_cli_audit.py` (15 — verify/list/tail/append × 3-4 scenarios each). The `EventKind.PURGE_PLAN` and `EventKind.PURGE_EXECUTE` payload slots were defined here in anticipation. **`O_APPEND` was chosen over tmp+fsync+rename** because tmp+fsync+rename is correct for whole-file writes but wrong for append-only logs — `rename()` would clobber prior records. POSIX guarantees that a single `write()` ≤ PIPE_BUF (4096 bytes) is atomic with `O_APPEND`; a JSONL line is comfortably under that for every `EventKind` payload in v0.5. **Cross-platform note**: macOS + Linux behave identically here; Windows is currently unsupported for `O_APPEND` atomicity but Windows isn't a v0.5 target (no Phase-2 platform-matrix entry). **Phase 2 momentum**: five of fifteen v0.5 tasks now closed in this run; the gitignore + hooks + gates + audit chain forms the integrity-and-policy layer the purge subsystem (T-111+) will sit on top of. Profiles with empty `required_any` (e.g. `_core/*`) skipped from detection — they apply unconditionally, not via inference. Walk depth defaults to 1 (root + immediate subdirs); 0 = root-only. Algorithm: resolve extends chains in caller order, dedupe by profile name (first wins), iterate each profile's `patterns_for_stage(stage)` filtering globally-seen lines, emit one `# --- name (display_name) ---` section per profile that contributed lines, prepend a provenance header (generated_at + stage + profiles + extends chain). Deterministic for fixed inputs. `VALID_STAGES = ("dev", "prod")` published for callers. **`swap.py`** (~310 lines) — `GitignoreSwap` with **SIGKILL-safe four-phase journal-then-write pattern** per §6.5: Phase 1 PREPARE → tmp+fsync+rename a recovery journal at `.sange/.recovery/swap-<utc>.json` (carrying old content + planned content + sha256 + phase=prepared); Phase 2 WRITE → tmp+fsync+rename the new `.gitignore`; Phase 3 ACTIVATE → tmp+fsync+rename `.sange/.active-profile`; Phase 4 COMMIT → delete the journal. Each phase update is itself a tmp+fsync+rename of the journal file. `recover()` walks `.sange/.recovery/` at session start, parses each journal, rolls forward from its recorded phase. `_atomic_write` uses `tempfile.mkstemp` (O_EXCL) → `os.fsync` → `os.replace` so a partial write never reaches the target path. **End-to-end smoke**: (a) normal swap on an empty repo writes `.gitignore` + `.sange/.active-profile`, journal cleaned up; (b) manually planted journal at `phase=prepared` (no `.gitignore` on disk), `recover()` writes both artifacts and cleans the journal. **Editorial discipline**: every claim in the docstrings verified by smoke (or unit test) before commit; the journal's tmp+fsync+rename pattern is the same one used by `_lib/output.py::_atomic_write` for generators, so the durability semantics already had prior art in the codebase. **Tests**: +49 across four files. test_gitignore_profile.py (16 — load_profile happy + 8 error paths + invariants + patterns_for_stage). test_gitignore_registry.py (14 — load, shadowing, bad-toml skipped, by_category, extends resolution, cycle detection, missing-parent, default_registry_roots smoke, all-36-shipped-load smoke). test_gitignore_compose.py (10 — single profile dev/prod, extends, dedup, error cases, determinism). test_gitignore_swap.py (9 — happy path, atomicity-no-tmp-files-leak, recover-from-each-phase, multiple-swaps-replace). **Two mid-implementation polish iterations**: (1) test fixture initially didn't pass `version=` to direct `Profile(...)` constructors — fixed by making `display_name` and `version` default-to-empty in the dataclass; `__post_init__` fills `display_name` from `name` when omitted, matching the loader's behavior. | S-003-T-80 | 2026-05-16T19:00Z | model | docs-fix | **GHCR public-visibility flip verified + `slsa-and-sbom.md` verification commands corrected to ones that actually work.** User confirmed off-screen that GHCR settings have been applied. Anonymous-pull probe: token endpoint returns 200, manifest endpoint returns 200, `docker pull ghcr.io/simsange/sange:v0.1.0.post1` succeeds without `docker login`, container `--version` reports `sange 0.1.0.post1`, `doctor` all 5 checks pass against the published image. **THEN: supply-chain verification commands were lying.** The doc I wrote in S-003-T-70 said to run `cosign verify ghcr.io/simsange/sange:v0.1.0` + `cosign verify-attestation --type slsaprovenance ...` — but **neither command works** against this release because `docker/build-push-action@v7`'s `provenance: true` writes attestations as **BuildKit in-index manifests** (the `unknown/unknown` entry the user spotted earlier), NOT via the cosign tag-prefix pattern (`<image>.sig` / `<image>.att`) that `cosign verify` / `cosign verify-attestation` / `slsa-verifier verify-image` discover by default. Empirically verified all three return `no signatures found` / `no matching attestations` / `SLSA verification failed`. **The attestations ARE real and ARE there** — proven via `docker buildx imagetools inspect ghcr.io/simsange/sange:v0.1.0.post1 --format '{{json .Provenance}}'`: SLSA v1.0 payload with `builder.id = github.com/simsange/sange/actions/runs/25933731407/attempts/1`, `buildType = buildkit/slsa-definitions`, `configSource.path = Dockerfile`, real `startedOn` + `finishedOn` timestamps from the v0.1.0.post1 release run. **Doc fix**: replaced the broken cosign block in `docs/security/slsa-and-sbom.md::How to verify a release` with two pieces — (1) "attestation readout" using `docker buildx imagetools inspect ... --format '{{json .Provenance}}'` + `... --format '{{json .SBOM}}'`, both verified to work; (2) a new "Why cosign verify-attestation doesn't find them" honesty section explaining the BuildKit-vs-cosign storage mismatch + pointing at the v0.5+ option of running `cosign attest --predicate ...` as a separate step in release.yml. Investigated: it's the OCI manifest carrying the SLSA provenance + CycloneDX SBOM attestations referencing the two real platform manifests as their `subject`. OCI spec says attestation manifests declare `platform: { architecture: "unknown", os: "unknown" }` because they're not platform-specific. **NOT a bug** — it's exactly the artifact `cosign verify-attestation` reads. Three options considered: (A) leave it, (B) switch to OCI 1.1 referrers via `cosign attest`, (C) drop attestations entirely. Option B requires a meaningful rewrite of release.yml + breaks single-tag `cosign verify-attestation <image>` syntax for consumers (forcing `--attachment-tag-prefix`). Option C kills the SLSA L3 + SBOM claims documented in slsa-and-sbom.md — a real regression. Picked **Option A + documentation**: added a "Why GHCR shows `unknown/unknown`" section to `docs/security/slsa-and-sbom.md` (~30 lines) explaining the artifact + why dropping it would defeat the supply-chain claims + the OCI 1.1 referrers alternative + the wait-for-GHCR-UI-to-catch-up posture. **The right fix here was documentation**, not config — every alternative loses real properties. Pytest 1168 still passing. **What's NOT pushed yet**: this session-log row + the slsa-and-sbom.md update. But `extract_body()` (called by the verifier) returns a body that includes the trailing newline. Result: any generator whose body didn't already end with `\n` produced an `output_sha256` claim that the verifier couldn't reproduce. The kit_manifest body was the first to surface this — it ends with prose, not a `bullet_list` which auto-adds `\n`. The other 5 generators happened to end with `bullet_list` and thus accidentally avoided the bug. ❌ Refuted (`output_sha` computed before trailing-`\n` normalization). ✅ Verified (`output_sha` now computed AFTER `if not canonical.endswith("\n"): canonical += "\n"`). All previously-written generator files were re-emitted with corrected hashes; `verify_generated.py` is now clean for all 7 implemented generators.
